- **Decided:** the exits' rules reach the kept parts (`KeptObject.mappingKept`, `KeptList.mappingKept`): a kept part with a sealed code in it, or one that says it is secret, never goes into a backup; a card number in a kept part is masked as the record is saved, and a recurrence's and a document framing's JSON are now masked like a category's (`FreeText.json`), since what a newer build wrote there may be words. *Alternative:* leave them out of the exits as machine text; a newer build's note inside a rule could then carry a card number into the binder and its backups.
- **Decided:** reading stays one decode of the type (`KeptObject.value(in:)`, `KeptList.items(in:)`); the JSON is read beside it only when a value is written or carried by a backup. Models read and write these properties only through their accessors (`values`/`keptValues`, `customFacts`/`keptFacts`, `sections`/`keptSections`, `behavior`/`keptBehavior`, `recurrence`/`keptRecurrence`, `framing`/`keptFraming`); a new architecture rule, `stored-json-outside-model`, fails a write to one of them elsewhere, and `StoredObjectsTests` lists every JSON property in the schema. The database's shape and the `.dosar` format don't change: the same JSON, with more of it kept.
- **Why:** a single stored object was read whole and written back from this build's types, so a key a newer build added (a deadline's reminder setting, a category's new behaviour, a page's new framing choice) was dropped the next time this build saved that object, or exported or imported it.

### Every stored day is kept as a day
- **Decided:** database version 9 keeps every property that means a day as the day's ISO text (`StoredDay`, `2026-11-15`): a deadline's due day (`Obligation.dueDayISO`) and the day its snooze ends (`snoozedUntilISO`), the due days a completion closed and moved to (`HistoryEntry.cycleDueDayISO`, `nextDueDayISO`), a document's date and its last valid day (`Attachment.documentDayISO`, `validUntilISO`). Before, they were moments (noon of the day in the phone's time zone, a snooze the start of its day), read back in the phone's current time zone, so on a phone ten or more hours from where a day was written every due day and expiry showed a day off. Code reads and writes them only through the models' `CalendarDay` properties (`dueDay`, `snoozedUntil`, `cycleDueDay`, `nextDueDay`, `documentDay`, `validUntil`); nothing outside the model converts. `setDueDay` and `dueDay(in:)` are gone, and so is the calendar argument of `Obligation`'s initializer. *Alternatives:* year, month and day as three integers (sorts and filters as well, but three properties per day and a form nothing else in the app uses); one integer `yyyyMMdd` (sorts, but a form of its own beside the archive's ISO days); keep moments and read them in a fixed time zone such as Bucharest (still a moment, and wrong the day the app counts in another zone).
- **Decided:** the ISO text sorts as the days do (four-digit years, zero-padded), so fetches sort by it (`Obligation.byDueDay`, lexical) and a `#Predicate` can compare it (`$0.dueDayISO <= cutoff`); the only fetch that sorted by the old moment (`ObligationSnapshot.fetchAll`, which reminders, widgets, Siri and Spotlight read) uses it. A stored text that isn't a day reads as none; a deadline with none is due on the day it was made.
- **Decided:** the moments that stay moments are when something happened, was made, last changed or was made smaller (`occurredAt`, `recordedAt`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `compactedAt`), and `DaysStoredAsDaysTests` lists every one, so a new day stored as a `Date` fails it. A visit's day (`occurredAt`) stays a moment: it is when the visit happened, and readings and change times compare with it.
- **Decided:** the upgrade is one custom stage (`DayMigration`): before the store changes shape it reads each moment as the day it meant and keeps the days in memory by record (its id and a moment it keeps); after, it writes them into their new places and saves once (`UnitOfWork.performPrivate`), with no change times, since the days mean what they meant. The old moments are not kept in version 9. *Alternative:* an intermediate version keeping both forms, filled by a lightweight stage and emptied by the next (two versions and two frozen copies for one move).
- **Decided:** the time zone a moment was written in wasn't stored, so the upgrade reads it in the phone's time zone at the upgrade (`Calendar.dosar`). It is the one it was written in for nearly everyone (a phone in Romania upgrading in Romania), and noon keeps the day for any time zone up to eleven hours away; a snooze's end, stored as the start of its day, is read as the noon after it, so it keeps its day the same way. A day written on a phone twelve or more hours from where it upgrades can still read a day off once, and stays the day it reads from then on. *Alternatives:* Bucharest's time zone (wrong for a phone that lives elsewhere, and the app never assumed Romania for a moment); guessing the zone from the moment's time of day (noon in which zone is ambiguous across a day).
- **Decided:** the `.dosar` format is unchanged: archives already carried these as ISO days, and a snooze's end as a moment (its day's start where it was written), which the importer reads as a day the same way the upgrade does.
- **Decided:** a new repair step, `DayYearsStep` (*Date cu anul greșit*), at the end of the list, finds a day with a year that can't be (more than 200 years ahead, or before 1800) in a section's date fields and in every stored day. Before the app counted in one Gregorian calendar, a phone set to another calendar wrote the days it worked out itself in that calendar's years (2569 on a Buddhist one). A year 543 ahead of one between 1900 and a hundred years ahead is the Buddhist calendar's, whose months and days are the Gregorian ones: it is offered back, asking first and naming both days. Any other year that can't be (a Japanese era's, an Islamic, Hebrew or Persian year, whose months differ) can't be worked back safely, so the finding only says to check the date (`isFixable` false). *Alternatives:* fix years too low by adding 543 as well (an Islamic or Persian year, 1448 or 1405, would become a likely-looking but wrong day); convert from each other calendar (which one, and which Islamic variant, isn't known).

### A model's numbers are read by the one number reader
- **Decided:** every number in a model's answer, whichever model gave it, is read by `ModelQuestions.number(_:as:)`: a JSON number in its machine form (`RomanianNumber.canonical` of its shortest text, so 19.99 stays 19.99), text through `RomanianNumber.read` as printed on a document. Money and an invoice's lines now take "1.234,56" (before, an amount given as text that wasn't machine-formatted was dropped), a doubtful amount ("3,750") is marked "Verifică" as on the phone's own reading, and nothing detours through `Double`. `JSONValue.double` reads JSON numbers only. The `number-parser` check now also catches `Decimal(string: String(…))` and `Double(…)`/`Float(…)` of text. *Alternatives:* keep `Double` for JSON numbers and add the reader only for text (two paths, and floating point for every amount); decode JSON numbers as `Decimal` in `JSONValue` (changes every provider's decoding for one reader's sake).

### A kind's labels are in its tables, and the check sees every list
- **Decided:** a policy's series-and-number and policyholder rows are rows of the policy table (`CaptureKindProfile.policyLabels`), read by `CompositeLabels.policy` (with the green card's header) and `PolicyholderReader`; a contract's codes are the contract's table (`CaptureKindProfile.contractLabels`). Words the shared readers look for that are no one kind's are named tables in one file, `CaptureLexicon`: what is never a line item, the buyer's block, an issuer's prefixes (now folded, so "FURNIZOR:" and "Vânzător:" lose their label whatever their letters), the supply-address labels, what sits beside a policyholder's name, the identity words and the classifier's whole-word signals. `CompositeLabels` has a file of its own, with the words that introduce a part. The switched-off policy and invoice number readers moved to `ios/Legacy/Services/Capture/LegacyNumberReaders.swift`, outside the build. `labels-outside-their-table` now also fails a list named …Labels, …Words or …Prefixes anywhere in `Services/Capture` outside the table files (`CaptureKindProfile`, `CaptureLexicon`, `CompositeLabels`, `IdentityTemplates`). The tables hold the same wordings as before. *Alternatives:* keep each list beside its reader and widen the check's exceptions (the exceptions list only shrinks); a lexicon per reader (many small tables, the same problem in more files); put the shared lists in `commonLabels` (they aren't fields' labels, and the readers would mistake them for some).

### What a kind is and keeps is its profile's
- **Decided:** what a kind is, is declared once in its profile: `isBill` (replaces `deadlineIsOptIn` and `kind == .utilityInvoice`), `isIdentityDocument`, `essentialKeys`, `hasMachineReadableBack` (an identity card: the "add the back" offer and "Pe spatele actului scrie") and `readsPaymentCode` (bill, service invoice, any other paper). What a kind keeps (`CaptureKindProfile.allows`) is decided from its profile and templates: every field it is asked for is kept, a personal document keeps what its templates read, its zone's values where its templates have one, its holder and its variant; other kinds keep what belongs to no other family, as before. A test pins that every asked field is kept for every kind. What changes: a cazier is asked for its expiry, so an expiry a model reads off one is kept (its field already says "unless one is given"); a driving licence no longer keeps a nationality, sex, document code or issuing country, which only a machine-readable zone gives and a licence has none. *Alternatives:* keep the hand-made key sets per kind and only add the test (two places to change for every new kind); a closed list of every kind's fields (drops fields the shared readers read today, a larger change to reading than asked).

### Values are compared one way
- **Decided:** codes, numbers, plates and names are compared by one helper, `RomanianText.compact` (folded, in capitals, letters and digits only), in place of eight copies, four of which didn't fold diacritics. Folding where it wasn't folded changes no stored identifier's match: POD, CLC and client codes, plates, IBANs and invoice numbers are letters and digits without diacritics, so they compare as before; a value read with a diacritic now finds the one kept without, and a name read as "Stefan" no longer rewrites the "Ștefan" the person typed (`CaptureFiler.merging`). A new check, `compact-outside-romanian-text`, fails a hand-written `uppercased().filter { $0.isLetter || $0.isNumber }`. *Alternatives:* keep the copies that don't fold (two answers to "is this the same code"); fold only in capture (the matcher and the plate formatter would still disagree with it).

### Apple's model asks one question per session, within its context
- **Decided:** the on-device model asks the kind and the facts each in a fresh session, so the facts question no longer carries the kind exchange; each carries as much of the text as its budget leaves (`OnDevicePromptBudget`): the model's context (`SystemLanguageModel.contextSize`, 4,096 before iOS 27), less a tenth, the instructions, the schema, the question and room for the answer (40 tokens a field, 24 a line of an invoice). From iOS 26.4 the system's tokenizer counts (`tokenCount(for:)`); before, an estimate of two and a half characters a token, the schema by its JSON encoding. At most 6,000 characters, cut at a line's end. A question with no room for text isn't asked; one that still runs out of room is asked once more with half the text, then fails calmly as before. *Alternatives:* one session with a smaller fixed cut (still overflows on a long policy's schema, and wastes context on newer phones); summarise the text first (a third question, slower, and the model may drop the values asked for); cap the answer with `maximumResponseTokens` (a long answer fails instead of fitting).

### Change times have one writer, and the app's own steps don't count
This replaces part of "One merge rule for every record" (2026-11-09): a step no longer sets a change time itself.
- **Decided:** `ChangeTimes`, run by the unit of work as it saves, is the only code that writes `updatedAt` (beside the importer, which writes the archive's times, and a model's initializer, which gives the moment it is made until the unit saves it). The hand-written stamps in the actions, the repair steps, `DeadlineReminders`, `TemplateStore` and the models' setters are gone. An action with a clock passes it to the unit (`UnitOfWork.perform(in:now:)`, `performUndoable(in:now:)`, `save(_:now:)`); a unit opened without one takes the first a step inside it gives, else the moment it saves. A step that writes the same values again changes nothing, so it gives no time. The architecture check rejects any other assignment (`change-time-outside-change-times`). *Alternatives:* keep "a step that sets a time itself keeps it" next to the unit's rule, which is the second path that let mixed clocks in (a model's setter writing the wall clock under an action's test clock); stamp in each action and drop the unit's rule, which every new action would have to remember.
- **Decided:** a unit says whose change it saves (`ChangeOrigin`). The person's step (the default) gives new times. The app's own step (`.app`: installing and upgrading the built-in categories, the repair list, the household's own deadlines at launch) changes no record's time; what it makes has the time it was made. Inside the person's unit an app-made step is part of the person's step (a deadline's reminders following an edit). Undo (`.restoring`) gives none, as it puts the old times back. A backup's writer (`performPrivate`) gives none to what it changes itself (`HouseholdRules.reconcile` inside an import), so those records keep the archive's or the phone's time, and the deadline it makes has the time it was made. *Alternatives:* a flag on each record saying who changed it last, which is a schema change and travels in archives for no gain; stamping app-made changes with the unknown time, which would make a record the app touched look older than a copy nobody changed.
- **Decided:** a repair the owner approves is the app's own step too, not the person's. It corrects what an earlier version saved; stamping it would let it outrank a real change the person made on another phone before the repair, and lose that change with "Adaugă". Not stamping loses nothing: the repair list looks again after every import and finds it again. *Alternative:* count the owner's tap as their change, which is safe only when no other phone has a newer edit.
- **Decided:** a built-in category is the app's definition, the same on every phone. Only the person's choices for it (its order, whether it shows on the binder, its lock) count as a change of it, and one the catalog installs (at launch, or an import that adds the built-ins a backup didn't mention) starts at `ChangeTime.unknown`. So an app update no longer makes this phone's built-ins "newer" and drops the order and choices from another phone's backup. *Alternative:* keep stamping built-ins and let the importer always take a backup's order, which would overwrite a newer reorder made here.
- **Decided:** no repair step for built-ins stamped by earlier updates. A stored change time can't tell an update's stamp from the person's own choice made after it; resetting would make a real later choice lose to an older backup, silently. The effect fades on its own: updates no longer stamp, so the next choice on either phone decides. "Înlocuiește tot" was never affected. *Alternative:* reset built-ins whose order, visibility and lock equal the catalog's defaults, which still guesses wrong for a person who changed a choice and changed it back.
- **Decided:** every import has the repair list look again, in the archive service (`importPackage`, `importPackageAway`), not in the screen that started it. Launch's steps and that look log a failure with the persistence log (`PersistenceLog`: the step and the kind of error, never a value) instead of dropping it; the next step still runs. The architecture check's `silent-action-failure` now also covers the category store's changes, the repair list and `HouseholdRules`. *Alternative:* throw the repair list's failure from the import, which would report a backup that came in whole as failed.

### One copy of a household deadline
- **Decided:** when a household deadline is there twice (each phone made its own rental income declaration, and "Adaugă" brought both), `HouseholdRules.reconcile` keeps the copy furthest on: the later due day first (a declaration filed this year is due next year, so it wins over one still due and no reminder comes back for it), then the one with more history, then the smallest id, so every phone and every read order keeps the same one. The others' completions and documents move onto it before they go. It is the app's own step, so nothing it changes gets a change time. *Alternatives:* keep whichever copy the fetch returns first, as before, which could keep the one still due and flip with each backup; merge the copies field by field, which has no rule for two different due days; prefer the newest change time, which a snooze or an edit of the copy still due would win.

### More personal documents from a photo: the green card and the electronic signature
- **Decided:** two more personal documents are read from a photo as data, the way the cazier and the bank card are: each a capture kind (`greenCard`, `electronicSignature`), a kind profile naming its existing document type (`DocumentType.greenCard`, `eSignature`) and a template in `IdentityTemplates` read by the shared `IdentityDocumentReader`. Their reminders are the types' own, already in the catalog (a month and a week before; legal for the green card, financial for the signature); capture sets none. Both models are asked what the templates read, with their captions, through the profiles (`ModelQuestions`), with no list of their own.
- **Decided:** a green card is a personal document, filed under who it insures, as the app already treated it (a type in Acte, which *Pleci din țară* reads beside the RCA). Its layout is the Council of Bureaux model (each caption in the country's language and in English: 3 validity, 4 the number under "Country code / Insurer's code / Number", 5 the plate, 9 the policyholder's name and address, 10 the insurer), with BAAR's Romanian wordings; the English captions, printed on every card, are read too, since the Romanian ones weren't checked on a Romanian card. Its number is a policy's series and number (`CompositeValue.policy`); the header is named once (`CompositeLabels.greenCardNumber`) and read by the policy table and the template. The first day of cover is its start (`startDate`), not an issue date, and isn't kept on the document. *Alternative:* file it with the car, as a page of its RCA; the trip check and the type already treat it as a document of its own, and a card bought for a trip is often separate from the RCA's paper.
- **Decided:** the green card keeps the plate it names (`document.vehiclePlate`, only for a green card; built-in catalog version 16), and *Pleci din țară* counts a filed card for the car with that plate, or for any car when the card or the car has no plate written down. Before, any green card counted for every car. *Alternative:* no plate, which keeps one car's card counting for another.
- **Decided:** a personal document finds an existing section by its number only among personal documents, and nothing else is filed into a personal document by a value they share (`CaptureReview.otherDestination`). A green card may print its RCA's number, and now keeps its car's plate, so without this a green card could be filed into the car and an ITP report into the green card. *Alternative:* match a hint only against the field it maps to (`CaptureMapping.candidates`); a green card's plate would still match an ITP report's.
- **Decided:** an RCA policy that carries its green card (one PDF, the card as its last page) or mentions it stays the policy: where the RCA's own words weigh at least "RCA" and "răspundere civilă auto" together (`DocumentClassifier.policyWithItsGreenCard`), the green card isn't a kind of its own on that page. A green card alone never prints them. *Alternative:* halve the green card's weight, as an RCA does to any other policy; a card's title words outweigh a policy's even halved.
- **Decided:** two shapes no template could express are two formats of the shared reader, for any template: a name printed before its address under one caption (`IdentityFormat.nameBeforeAddress`: the words before the first comma, digit or address word, `PostalAddress.isMarker`; a firm by its legal form, `RomanianFieldExtractor.namesACompany`, is no one's name, so a leasing company's card asks who it belongs to instead of making a person of it), and a registration plate (`IdentityFormat.licensePlate`, through `RomanianFieldExtractor.licensePlate`). *Alternative:* read the holder as a plain name; on a photo the address stacked under it is read with it, and the name was lost.
- **Decided:** a qualified electronic signature certificate has no official layout (each trust service provider prints its own), so its labels are the wordings certificates are shown with, in Romanian and in English: the holder, the CNP when printed, the serial number as printed (pairs of hex digits, with spaces or colons), *Valabil de la … până la …* and the provider. It has no variants: how long it lasts is printed on it.
- **Unchanged (owner):** the national health card stays typed in only, with no template, as decided in "Bank cards stay on the phone; a cazier is typed in by its issue date": its printed fields still couldn't be confirmed against the official specimen (Ordinul CNAS nr. 520/2011 couldn't be read for this work either).
- **Why:** these were personal documents filed as a generic one, with the type picked by hand in Editează.

### The card rule leaves IBANs and long codes whole
- **Decided:** the "anywhere" rule (`PaymentCardNumbers.cardNumbers(in:)`) first leaves out of each run of digits what belongs to a code, then looks for a card in what is left (`pieces(of:in:outside:)`), as before: digits glued to a letter, before or after them, with nothing between (an IBAN's account in one piece, its first group after the country's letters, a code such as "AB4111…"), since a card number is never printed glued to letters; the groups of an IBAN that passes its own check, in one piece, in groups, in any case, with spaces or dashes (`FieldFormatter.ibanRanges`); and, where no IBAN passes, groups that carry on an IBAN printed in groups (a listed country's capitals and check digits, then groups of four: "RO49 AAAA 1234 …"), so a mistyped IBAN stays whole too. Whatever is left out of a run makes the rest a part of it, which must pass the Luhn check and be printed like a card. A card number beside an IBAN or a code with a space between is still masked ("RO49AAAA1B31007593840000 4111111111111111" keeps the IBAN and masks the card). *Alternatives:* leave out the whole run when any of it is glued to a letter, which also keeps a card printed after an IBAN with one space; treat any two letters and two digits as an IBAN's start, which keeps a card printed after "AB12 " (only countries that issue IBANs count now); try every length for an IBAN of a country not listed, where, by chance, about one start in five has a length that passes and would keep a card after a two-letter word ("cu", "de").
- **Decided:** inside a long number printed all in groups of four, a card is looked for only at its start, or right after a card found there ("4111 1111 1111 1111 0828", two cards in a row): every part of such a number is grouped like a card, so where the card starts has to show. A 20-digit number in five groups (a health card's) is never cut into one. Inside runs printed otherwise, the rules of "A card number printed next to other digits" stay. The forgiving of a misread check digit stays for a whole run of exactly sixteen digits in fours, standing on its own. *Alternative:* the start or the end of the run, which keeps a health card's last sixteen digits a candidate.
- **Decided:** a value whose field type says what it is stays as typed when it passes that type's own check (`PaymentCardNumbers.keepsWhole`, `typesKeptWhole`): an IBAN and a pet's microchip (ISO 11784, 15 digits, which a Romanian chip's 642 starts like a Maestro card). The save reads the section's fields only when something would be masked (`PaymentCardNumbers.sanitizing(_:fields:)`, given by `FreeText` from the section's category), and capture keeps a microchip it read (`CaptureFieldKey.typeKeptWhole`), so the section is still found by it. A card number typed into a microchip field fails its check (sixteen digits) and is masked. *Alternatives:* every typed field with a check (a phone, a VIN, a plate), but a phone's and a VIN's checks also let a card's digits through (up to 15 digits, 17 characters that may all be digits), and plates and VINs carry letters, which the glued rule already covers; no field exemption, which leaves one Romanian microchip in ten masked in its own field.
- **Decided:** an IBAN is checked at its country's length, as the ISO 13616 registry lists them (`FieldFormatter.ibanLengths`, used by `isValidIBAN` and the finder), with two letters and two check digits first. Before, only a Romanian IBAN's length was checked; a country not listed is still checked by its check digits alone in a field.
- **Measured** (20,000 samples per case, a seeded generator, the rule before and after; no real account numbers): a NatWest IBAN in one piece or in groups 10.0% masked → 0%; Lloyds 2.8% → 0%; a Lithuanian (Revolut) IBAN 3.0% → 0%; a French IBAN of a 300xx bank in groups 17.3% → 0%; a German ING IBAN in groups 10.4% → 0%; a Romanian IBAN in groups with the placeholder bank code 39.6% → 0% (and mistyped, 39.6% → 0%); a 20-digit health card number in groups 4.9% → 0%; a code glued to letters 4.0% → 0%; a 20-digit code in fours 7.9% → 3.9%; a 24-digit code in fours 11.7% → 4.1%. Unchanged, as it can't be told from a card by its digits alone: a 16-digit code standing alone, or after a word and a space, 4.0%; a microchip in plain text (a note), 10.0% (0% in its own field). Every published test number in the earlier cases and beside an IBAN is still masked.
- **Decided:** values an earlier build masked wrongly can't be put back (the digits are gone), so there is no repair step; an IBAN masked that way fails its field's check and is typed again.
- **Why:** since every save masks every record's words (`FreeText.mask`), the rule's mistakes became permanent: an IBAN that happened to hold a card-shaped run lost its digits, then failed its own field's check, so the section couldn't be saved until it was typed again, and "Anulează" brought back the masked text.

### Main actions pinned, by one modifier
- **Decided:** every screen's and sheet's main action is pinned with `.dsPinnedAction`, now also for the review's "Salvează", the visit form's "Salvează vizita" and the saved visit's "Gata", the deadline form's "Salvează termenul", "Am rezolvat"'s "Salvează", "Altă zi"'s "Adaugă", the backup password sheet, the trip's questions ("Vezi ce-ți trebuie"), the add-section steps, "Scriu eu", "Mută la…" and onboarding. The bar takes what goes with the action in one order: `note:` above it (why it can't be pressed yet, "Nu s-a salvat"), the action, `secondary:` under it ("Fă copia fără parolă", "Anulează vizita", "Renunță la schimbări"); `DSBottomBar` is private to the modifier, so a bar can't be drawn another way. Enabled state, `DSDelayedProgress` and keyboard behaviour stay as they were (the bar rides above the keyboard, as the hand-made ones did). *Alternative:* a note and other actions left in the scrolling content above a pinned button; an error said where the person isn't looking, and two places to look.
- **Decided:** the save error ("Nu s-a salvat") moves into the bar's note, beside the button that failed. *Alternative:* keep it at the end of the content; with the button pinned it could sit off screen.
- **Decided:** onboarding's buttons take the bar's material (`.bar`) instead of the page's background, so every pinned action looks the same. *Alternative:* an option for a plain background; a second look for the same thing.
- **Decided:** two architecture rules, read by following braces (strings and comments left out): `main-action-in-scroll` refuses a `DSPrimaryButton` written inside a `ScrollView`'s braces, in app screens and the share extension (the design-system gallery aside), and `hand-pinned-action` refuses a `safeAreaInset(edge: .bottom)` that draws `DSBottomBar`, `DSPrimaryButton`, `DSSecondaryButton` or `dsFullWidthButton`. No exceptions. What they can't see, said plainly: a primary button in a property or a subview that a `ScrollView` names (the trip's questions were found by reading, not by the rule), and a bottom inset that draws buttons passed in from outside (onboarding's was). A primary button inside a card at the top of a page (the deadline page's "Am rezolvat", "Sună", an empty state's action) isn't at the end of content and stays. The share practice's "Trimite factura de probă" stays in the content: it is step one of the steps above it, not the page's last action. *Alternative:* a rule on the last view of the content; braces don't say which view is last.
- **Decided:** a filing that takes time returns at once while one is running (`guard !isSaving` at the start of the review's, the visit form's and "Am rezolvat"'s save), so two taps before the button shows it is busy file once. The synchronous saves ("Scriu eu", the deadline form, "Mută la…") finish within the tap. *Alternative:* a guard inside `DSPrimaryButton`; the button only sees the state it was drawn with, so a second tap in the same frame passes it.
- **Why:** AGENTS already said a main action is never left at the end of scrolling content; three forms still did it and seven places drew the bar by hand.

### A paper's number is its issuer's
- **Decided:** the same number is the same paper for certain only when it can only be one issuer's: a bill within its subscription (one provider; unchanged), or an invoice from the same firm on the same day. Firms number their own invoices, and store receipts and services often start again each year: the same number from another firm (both named) is another paper, never joined; with a firm on one of them only, or on another day, it is `numberOnly`, which saving still treats as the paper already filed but offers "E altă factură" for, as for a match by day and total. The most certain match is taken first (the same number from the same issuer, the number alone, then the day and total). *Alternative:* a number alone is never a match; another photo of an invoice whose firm wasn't read would log a second visit again. *Alternative:* the same firm and the same number is certain on any day; a firm's numbering that starts again each year would silently update last year's visit.
- **Decided:** the lookup has one order for every paper, `PaperIdentity.filed(keptDocumentEntry:paper:among:calendar:)`: the same document's entry first (the caller says where that document's entry may be: any subscription for a bill, this section for a visit), then the rule, unless the person said it is another paper. The filing plan and the visit form both call it.
- **Decided:** one wording, `FiledPaperWording` (title, "Vezi factura", "E altă factură", and what saving does, for a bill and for an invoice, by match), and one component, `DSFiledPaperNotice(filed:paper:onOpen:onAnother:)`, in the review, the visit form and "Am rezolvat". "Am rezolvat" now offers "Vezi factura" too (it closes the sheet, then opens the bill). The meter's line moves to `CaptureFilingPlan.Meter.sentence`, read by the review and "Am rezolvat". *Alternative:* the wording as statics on one screen, borrowed by the others; the third screen had already lost "Vezi factura".
- **Why:** a different invoice with the same number replaced an earlier visit without a question, and the notice was drawn three times.

### Visits logged twice are joined from the repair list
- **Decided:** a new step at the end of the registry, `SameVisitTwiceStep` (`same-visit-twice`, *Vizite trecute de mai multe ori*), modelled on `SameBillTwiceStep`: on each section, visits, repairs and purchases that each have a paper and that `PaperIdentity` says are the same invoice for sure (`number` or `dayAndTotal`) are offered as one finding per group ("Factura din 30 mai 2026 e trecută de mai multe ori în istoricul „VW Golf 2018”. O las o singură dată?"). With the owner's OK, in one unit of work: the visit whose paper came last stays; every paper moves to it, the newest current and the others archived beside it; it takes what it lacks from the others (number, total, firm, rating, note, deadline); their parts and jobs it doesn't list (by component, else by name); one reading (its own, else the other's, moved to its moment), the others' readings removed and the hero worked out again. A finding that no longer holds changes nothing; once joined nothing is found. Examples are left out. *Alternative:* join `numberOnly` too; that is the case where numbering may have started again, so it is left for the person to see in Istoric. *Alternative:* compare visits typed by hand; they were never doubled by a photo, and two jobs on one day for the same amount can be real.
- **Why:** before another photo of a visit's invoice was known as the same invoice, filing it again made a second visit, and nothing repaired those.

### Recent searches remember only what search may keep
- **Decided:** one rule decides what the person's words may be kept as on this phone outside the binder, `SearchPrivacy.mayRemember(_:)`: only text that search's own redaction leaves as it is, so nothing with a CNP-shaped number, a card number (whole by `PaymentCardNumbers`, or already masked) or a sealed value. Recent searches use it when a search is added (with the old rule that a search which found anything in a locked section is never kept, `mayRemember(_:results:)`) and again when the list is read, so an entry an earlier version kept goes, from the list and from the phone. *Alternative:* add a card-number check beside the CNP check in `RecentSearches`; the list would then have its own idea of what search keeps, free to drift from the index's.
- **Decided:** a trip's destination, the one line of a kept plan in the person's words, follows the same rule: one that search wouldn't keep is left out (the trip itself is still kept), when it is saved and when it is read back. *Alternative:* forget the whole plan; the person would lose the dates and travellers over one line.
- **Decided:** the other stores outside the database keep what they keep. The widget snapshot carries the binder's own titles, already masked when they were saved (`FreeText`), with a generic title for a locked category; the share inbox holds what was shared, as it was shared, until it is filed or dismissed, and its names are shown only through `SearchPrivacy.outsideText`, its reading through `InboxPreviewer`; the Spotlight ledger keeps fingerprints only; outside failures, widget completions, the box order and settings hold no words of the person's. Drafts live in memory only.

### One JSON coder for machine text
- **Decided:** machine JSON is written by `MachineText.jsonEncoder(_:)` and read by `MachineText.jsonDecoder()` (in `ios/Shared`), for the widget snapshot, the share inbox's manifests and previews, a widget's completions, outside failures and `.dosar` archives. Two layouts, by name: `.compact` (keys sorted, one line) for what the app and its extensions keep and hand each other, `.readable` (indented, keys sorted, slashes as they are) for archives. Both write a moment in UTC to the second, byte for byte what each wrote before (Foundation's `.iso8601` and the archive's `ISO8601FormatStyle` give the same text); tests pin both layouts against the coders they replace. The one reader takes what the archive reader already took (fractional seconds, any UTC offset, a day alone), a superset of what the others read, so nothing kept before stops reading. *Alternative:* keep each store's own strategy; seven copies already differed in what they read, and a store that met an archive's form would have thrown the file away.
- **Decided:** the widget snapshot is one `Codable` type, `WidgetFeed`, in `ios/Shared`: the app builds it (`WidgetFeed.make`, in the app because it reads the attention model) and writes it (`WidgetSnapshotWriter`), the widget reads it. A tint and a kind travel by their stored names, since the widget can't link the app's models; a due day is a `CalendarDay`. The app's copy, `WidgetSnapshot`, is gone. *Alternative:* keep the mirror with a round-trip test; the contract would still be written twice.
- **Decided:** the version-2 archive's own coder factory (`dosarArchiveEncoder` and `dosarArchiveDecoder`) is removed: nothing wrote or read with it, version-2 archives have been read through the version-3 reader since it existed.
- **Decided:** a new `json-date-coding` check fails on a date strategy anywhere in the app, its widgets, the share extension and `ios/Shared` outside `MachineText.swift`. Values stored as JSON on models (`StoredJSON`) set no date strategy and are left as they are: their bytes are stored data.

### One bitmap canvas, and widgets' Lock Screen line
- **Decided:** a picture is drawn on one bitmap canvas, `ImageScaling.canvas(width:height:onWhite:)` (sRGB, no alpha; white first when asked). `ImageScaling` sizes on it, and `DocumentPicture` turns a page and `PDFPageRendering` draws a PDF page on it, white underneath; `PageTurn.canvas` is gone. A new `own-canvas` check fails on a `CGContext(`, `UIGraphicsImageRenderer` or `UIGraphicsBeginImageContext` outside `ImageScaling.swift`; a PDF being written (`CGContext(consumer:)`, a share's PDF) is another job and is left out. *Alternative:* let `DocumentPicture` keep a canvas of its own as the one drawer AGENTS names; two canvases with the same settings would again be free to drift.
- **Decided:** a new `view-that-fits` check fails on `ViewThatFits` in the app, the share extension and `ios/Shared`, and leaves the widgets out by its path, with the reason beside it: a Lock Screen line (`accessoryInline`) is one line of text the system lays out itself, ignoring any layout of the app's, and takes a shorter wording only through `ViewThatFits`; `DSAdaptiveStack` lives in the app's design system and isn't built into the widget. *Alternative:* list the widget's line as an exception; the list only shrinks, and this is a rule of the platform, not a debt.

### The cover by the scene's phase, and time away on a clock that only counts
- **Decided:** `LockCover.decide` reads where the scene is (`LockCover.Phase`: active, inactive, background) instead of only "active or not". With the app lock on, or a section lock on and the session open, the app is covered whenever it is in the background, whatever a Face ID prompt says, since that is when iOS takes the app switcher's picture; and while inactive, except during a Face ID prompt, since the system's prompt itself makes the app inactive (never background) and must not sit under the app's own cover. Active is never covered by the switcher cover; the app lock with the session closed covers in every phase, as before. A phase a newer iOS adds counts as the background. The whole table (every lock setting, every phase, prompt or not) and the sequences a person goes through (a prompt, the switcher during a prompt, the switcher and back, home) are pinned in `LockCoverDecisionTests`. *Alternative:* keep "not active" with the prompt as an exception and close the prompt as the app leaves; the prompt's answer arrives on its own time, so the cover would still wait for it. *Alternative:* cover during the prompt too; the person would see the app's cover under the system's sheet each time they open a code.
- **Decided:** the time away that closes the session after a minute is counted on `LockClock`, which reads `ContinuousClock`: it keeps counting while the phone sleeps, and the phone's date, time and time zone settings don't move it. Setting the phone's clock back or forward while away changes nothing; time away starts the first time the app goes to the background and ends when it comes back. `relockAfterBackground` is a `Duration` (60 seconds, as before). Tests give the vault a clock they move themselves. *Alternative:* `SuspendingClock` (or `ProcessInfo.systemUptime`, `mach_absolute_time`): they stop while the phone sleeps, so a night with the screen off would count as almost nothing. *Alternative:* `clock_gettime(CLOCK_MONOTONIC_RAW)` by hand; on Apple platforms it counts sleep too (it is what `ContinuousClock` reads), but `ContinuousClock` is the typed, documented way to it. *Alternative:* keep `Date` and treat a clock that went back as "long away"; still wrong for a clock set forward, and two rules for one minute. The session lives only in memory, so a restart, which starts the clock again, already closes it.
- **Decided:** the vault has no other timing. A shown secret code hides after 30 seconds through `Task.sleep`, which already counts on `ContinuousClock`, and at once when the app leaves the screen.
- **Why:** iOS takes the switcher's picture when the app goes to the background, whatever a prompt is doing, so that is when the cover must be whole; and a minute away should be a minute whatever the phone's clock says.

### One month arithmetic
- **Decided:** a day moves by months in one place, `CalendarDay.adding(months:dayOfMonth:calendar:)` (in `ios/Shared/CalendarDay.swift`), in the app's calendar: the same day, or the target month's last day when it is shorter, and with `dayOfMonth` the day a series keeps instead of the day's own. A month's length is asked only of the calendar, `CalendarDay.daysInMonth(calendar:)`. Repeating deadlines (`RecurrenceEngine.add`, days and weeks too), reminder leads in months and the travel cut-off (`ReminderLead`), a month-day in a given year (`MonthDay.day(inYear:)`), a day checked from outside (`CalendarDay(validYear:month:day:)`) and capture's lengths (`printed(addingMonths:)`, unchanged: it already went through `CalendarDay`) all use it. *Alternative:* keep `CadenceCalculator.addMonths` and make only its month table read the calendar; there would still be two ways to move a day by months (one on moments keeping the time of day, one on days), free to drift on the next change.
- **Decided:** `CadenceCalculator` keeps only the counter projections. Its calendar half (`isLeapYear`, its own Gregorian month table, `addMonths`, `addYears`, `calculateNextDueDate` and `CalendarCadenceInterval`) is removed, not switched off: `RecurrenceEngine` was its only caller, every result is the same for every day (checked against Foundation's own month arithmetic for every day from 2023 to 2029, -30 to +30 months, in five time zones), and its tests move to `MonthArithmeticTests` with the same days in and out. *Alternative:* keep it as a thin wrapper on moments for the tests alone; an unused second door is where a second definition comes back.
- **Decided:** a new architecture rule, `month-length` (with fixtures, no exceptions), rejects a table of month lengths (`31, 28, 31`), a switch over the 30- and 31-day months, a leap-year rule (`isLeapYear`, `% 400`, `% 100 != 0`, `year % 4`) and asking the calendar for a month's length anywhere in app code but `CalendarDay.swift`. Rates counted per 30 days (a car's kilometres a month), reminder leads stored as 30 or 365 days and the first version's cadences in days are amounts, not month arithmetic, and stay. *Alternative:* catch only literal tables; a hand-written leap rule is the same second calendar.
- **Decided:** the date pickers in the visit form and "Mută termenul" lose their own `.environment(\.locale, .dosarDefault)`: the root's `.dosarLocaleAndCalendar()` already gives them the locale and the calendar, and sheets inherit both.
- **Why:** month ends and 29 February were worked out twice, once by the calendar and once by a hand-written Gregorian table; both agreed today, and one source of truth keeps them from disagreeing later. Nothing a person sees changes.

### Every kind's labels are its profile's table
- **Decided:** what a kind prints its values under is data in its profile (`CaptureKindProfile.labels`), for every kind, not only bills: a policy's period of cover, its end, its claims line and its class (RCA and any other policy share one table, `policyLabels`); an ITP report's next inspection (`inspectionLabels`); a vet's microchip, vaccine and booster (`veterinaryLabels`); a receipt's or warranty's day of sale, warranty and serial number (`purchaseLabels`). What every document prints the same way is one shared table, `CaptureKindProfile.commonLabels`: the issue date, the total (strongest wording first, a policy's premium among them), a VIN printed in groups and the odometer. `labels(for:)` gives a kind's own rows for a key, all of them, else the shared row, so a kind can word a shared value its own way by adding a row. Invoices (service, unknown documents) and medical results have no rows of their own: everything they label is shared.
- **Decided:** the readers stay shared and are told the table (`RomanianFieldExtractor.labelledDate`, `validityPeriod`, `claimsPhone`, `bonusMalus`, `microchip`, `serialNumber`, `warrantyMonths`, `odometer`, `vin`, `total` take `labels:`; a pattern that needs the labels builds them in as one group, in the table's order, so the readings are the same as before). What isn't a kind's label stays the shared reader's, named beside it: the plain "total" and the words that make a "total" line some other sum, the buyer's and the issuer's words, a Romanian chip's country prefix (642). A new wording is a new row, read with no code; a test adds a wording to each kind's table and sees it read (`CaptureAnalyzer.reading(labelsOf:)`). A new architecture rule, `labels-outside-their-table` (with fixtures), refuses a list of labels written where capture reads them (`labelledDate([…])`, `lines(containing: […])`, `labels: […]`, `let labels = […]`); no exceptions.
- **Decided (cautious):** a bill keeps its own issue-date wordings, read as a layout by `BillReader`, and only when they find none is the date read as every document labels it; the bill's table gets no shared rows, so what the bill reader sees is unchanged.
- **Decided:** reading results stay as they were. The other kinds' rows are read by the line readers they had (a date after the label on its line or the next; the phone on the label's line or the next; the vaccine as the line that names it), not yet by `LabelledLayout` as a bill's are. *Alternative:* read every kind's table with `LabelledLayout`, the way identity documents and bills are, which keeps values in their columns on a photo but matches labels as whole words (so "valabil" would stop finding "valabilă" until its wordings are written out) and never takes a value from a line that is another label; that changes what is read and is worth doing once ITP reports, vet records and policies have been read from real photos. *Alternative:* one table per kind with the shared rows copied in (more rows to keep alike).
- **Why:** the last "Kind profiles" gap: these kinds' labels were lists written inside the analyzer and its helpers, so a new wording meant new code, and nothing kept a reader from growing a list of its own.

### One app calendar
- **Decided:** the app, its widgets and the share extension count days in one calendar, `Calendar.dosar` (in `ios/Shared/DosarCalendar.swift`, beside `Locale.dosarDefault`): Gregorian, in Romanian (weeks start on Monday), in the phone's time zone. Every default `calendar:` parameter (`CalendarDay`, `MonthDay`, recurrence, urgency, reminders, wording, capture's filing, archives, actions), every "today" and every day made from a moment goes through it; `Calendar.dosar(in:)` gives it in a set time zone, and the UTC calendar for days alone (`CalendarDay.isoCalendar`) and `DosarFormat`'s writer are made by it too. Screens that read the environment's calendar, and the date pickers, get it from `.dosarLocaleAndCalendar()`, set where the locale was set before (the app's window, the lock's window) and now on the share sheet too. *Alternative:* keep `Calendar.current` and only force Gregorian where a day is written; a Buddhist phone would then still add a month to the wrong year's February and compare years 543 apart.
- **Decided:** the time zone is the phone's, read on each use, as `Calendar.current` read it, so travelling keeps counting days where the phone is (the made calendar is kept until the time zone changes, so asking costs nothing). The environment's copy is made again when the system says the time zone changed and whenever the app comes back to the front. *Alternative:* `Calendar.autoupdatingCurrent`'s own following, which follows the phone's calendar too, the thing this removes.
- **Decided:** a new architecture rule, `app-calendar` (with fixtures), rejects `Calendar.current`, `Calendar.autoupdatingCurrent`, a `.current` calendar default or argument and `Calendar(identifier:)` anywhere in app code but that one file; no exceptions. Tests keep the explicit calendars they build (Bucharest, UTC, other zones). *Alternative:* reject only `Calendar.current`; a hand-made Gregorian calendar elsewhere is a second path that drifts (no locale, no first weekday).
- **Why:** on a phone set to another calendar, a day's year, month and day numbers were counted in that calendar, while every document, deadline and archive is Gregorian; and a field's latest year ("this year plus one") read 2570 on a Buddhist phone. Nothing changes on a phone set to the Gregorian calendar.

### Capture reads into the app's own day, money and role
- **Decided:** capture keeps no copies of the app's value types. A day read off a page is a `CalendarDay`, an amount a `Money`, and the role a field fills a template's `FieldRole`, from the first reader to the database; `CaptureDay`, `CaptureMoney` and `CaptureFieldRole` are gone, and so are the conversions between them (`CaptureMapping.calendarDay`, `.money`, the visit form's day turned into a `Date` and back). Capture runs only in the app (the share extension hands what it gets to the inbox and doesn't link capture), so it can use `Money` and `FieldRole` where they are; nothing had to move into `ios/Shared`. *Alternative:* move `Money` into `ios/Shared` as well; not needed while the extension never reads a document, and it would carry `RomanianNumber` with it.
- **Decided:** what only capture's day knew stays, as one rule each. A day from outside the app is checked against the calendar once, for everyone (`CalendarDay(validYear:month:day:)`: no 31 February, no month 13). The years a document prints (1900 to 2200, so "0031" is a misread and not a day) are capture's, beside its date windows (`CalendarDay(printedYear:month:day:)` in `CaptureConsistency`), and so is a length in months worked out from a page (`printed(addingMonths:)`: the month's last day when it is shorter, Gregorian whatever the phone's calendar, and nothing for a length no document states). *Alternative:* put the years in `CalendarDay` itself; they are a reading rule, not a property of a day, and a day typed by hand can fall outside them.
- **Decided:** capture's role list loses what nothing read: the key facts and the title it listed never filled anything, since a key fact goes only where the template names it (`CaptureMapping.candidates`). What is left maps a capture field straight to the template's role (`CaptureMapping.role(for:)`). An amount's currency is written in capitals as soon as it is read (`Money` does it), so two readers that differ only in the case of "RON" agree. No database or archive change: a capture result was never stored.
- **Why:** two types for one day meant every screen between reading and filing turned one into the other, and each turning was a place for a day to slip in a time zone.

### Capture's retry waits a named backoff
- **Decided:** a Vision pass that fails waits `VisionCaptureRecognizer.retryPause` (300 ms, as before) and is read once more; a reading cancelled during that wait isn't read again. It is a backoff, not motion, so it is named beside the reader and not in `DS.Motion`: Vision says nothing when it is ready again, so there is no event to wait for. The `guessed-pause` check now covers the whole app, services included, with its fixture on a service and no exceptions. *Alternative:* `DS.Motion`, which is for what a person sees move; or wait for the model to load first, which Vision doesn't offer.
- **Why:** this finishes "No pause waits a set time on screen", which left the retry and the services for this work.

### Apple's on-device model asks what the kind's profile lists
- **Decided:** Apple's on-device model is asked exactly the fields the kind's profile lists (`CaptureKindProfile.expectedKeys`), as a generation schema built for that kind when it is asked (`OnDeviceSchema`, Foundation Models' `DynamicGenerationSchema`), one optional property per field. Each field is described once for both models (`ModelQuestions`: its name, meaning, type and format, a personal document's printed labels), and the AI provider's schema is every kind's questions, the guess's first (`CloudSchema`, unchanged in what it asks). A field's format is the key's own, whatever the document (`ModelQuestions.format(of:)`), and a test checks that every identity template writes its fields the same way. The hand-written list the on-device model asked (`GeneratedDocumentFacts`) is gone. *Alternative:* keep one fixed `@Generable` list covering every kind, which asks a bill for a VIN and a policy for line items, and drifts from the profiles each time a kind gains a field.
- **Decided:** the model is asked what the document is first, in the same session, and only while the kind is still open (`ReadingArbiter.kindIsOpen`: the classifier is unsure and nothing deterministic backs it), since otherwise its answer would change nothing; then it is asked the facts of the kind it named (`ReadingArbiter.openKind`; "Altul" is no opinion), else of the phone's kind. *Alternatives:* one question with the kind and the guess's facts together, which asks the wrong kind's facts exactly when the guess is wrong; asking every kind's facts, as the AI provider is, which the on-device model's small context doesn't fit.
- **Decided:** one mapper reads every model's answer (`ModelAnswerMapper`, which takes over from `CloudAnswerMapper` and the on-device model's own mapping), by each field's format, with the same checks (a CNP's and a VIN's control digit, dates in their windows, an address that names a place, a direct debit stated and not offered, the last four digits of a card and never more, a warranty of 1 to 240 months, now for the provider too). What a value is worth follows what the reader saw (`ModelQuestions.Reader`): from the page, as before; from the phone's text alone, a value is kept only when it is printed in that text, issuer and product names included (before, those two were kept unprinted), and only the invoice lines printed in it; a date is kept only when the text prints it, not merely because it falls inside its window. A value printed in parts counts as printed when each part is ("Seria RO/00/X00/XX nr. 012345678"), for both readers. On-device values keep their old worth (0.72, always marked "Verifică"); its days, which were 0.65 without being checked against the text, are now 0.72 and printed. *Alternative:* keep the on-device model's looser rule for names, which kept a firm it made up.
- **Decided:** what the on-device model reads stays as it was: the recognised text, on the phone, its first 6,000 characters; its availability is read the same way, and every capture still leaves the engine through `CaptureResult.withoutCardNumbers`.
- **Why:** the on-device model asked its own list, so a kind's profile, the review's added fields and the AI provider's questions could say one thing and the model another.

### No pause waits a set time on screen
- **Decided:** the lock window goes when its fade out has completed (`withAnimation` with `completionCriteria: .removed`, the design system's fade, a short one with Reduce Motion), not 400 ms after it started. Each uncovering carries its own token, so covering again during the fade keeps the cover whatever the old fade does next. A cover taken away before it was ever drawn has nothing to fade and goes at once. *Alternative:* attach the completion to the transaction and let the layer's own `dsAnimation` drive the fade; whether a completion follows an animation added below it isn't something to rely on, so the controller makes the fade it waits for.
- **Decided:** covering is never animated. The lock and the app-switcher cover are whole from their first frame, and switch between each other at once, where the lock used to fade in over the switcher cover (a cross-fade lets the app show through halfway). Uncovering fades both out; the switcher cover used to vanish at once. *Alternative:* fade in as well, quickly; any fade in shows the app through the cover for some frames.
- **Decided:** De verificat's landing waits for a route that comes with the app's opening, and gives way the moment one comes (`AppNavigator.routeArrives(within:)`); a route asked for while the app was away wins at once, as before. A tapped reminder or link reaches the app while iOS brings it onto the screen, and nothing says when nothing will, so that one wait has a bound: the opening's own length, `DS.Motion.appOpening` (400 ms, the owner's beat, now named for what it covers). *Alternative:* land as soon as the app is active and let a later route take over; that switches tabs under the person when a reminder's tap comes a moment after. *Alternative:* wait for the shell's sheet queue or the tab's stack; neither tells whether a route is still coming, and something open over the tabs already stops the landing.
- **Decided:** a new check, `guessed-pause`: in screen code (the app's views, navigation, intents and models, the widgets, the share extension and `ios/Shared`) a sleep or delay with a number in it fails. The four left were named for what they are: the search's typing debounce, a firm's notes debounce, how long "Copiat" stays under a secret row, and the share practice's look in the inbox, which only covers a signal iOS didn't deliver. Services name their own durations and stay outside the rule for now; the capture reader's 300 ms retry is still a number, left for the capture work. *Alternative:* the whole app at once, with that line listed as an exception, which only the owner can add.
- **Why:** a guessed pause is too short on a slow phone or with Reduce Motion, and too long everywhere else. This replaces the "Left" note of "Screens wait for the event, not for a pause".

### The same paper filed again, wherever it comes in
- **Decided:** one rule knows a paper already in the binder, a bill or a visit's invoice alike: `PaperIdentity` (it was `BillIdentity`). Within the section it belongs to, its number when both have one, else its day and total together, and the firm that issued it when both name one (the same day and amount from another firm is another invoice). A bill names no firm (its subscription already is its provider), so nothing changes for bills. The review, "Am rezolvat", the visit form, the filer and the repair list all use it; what each shows is one `FiledPaper`. *Alternative:* a second rule for visits with the firm always required, which would be two rules for one question and would never match a visit typed without a firm.
- **Decided:** a visit's, a repair's or a purchase's invoice is known on its own section, among its records that aren't bills or a deadline's completion (`PaperIdentity.visits(of:)`). The visit form reads the invoice's number and keeps it on the visit (`HistoryEntry.reference`, already there since version 7, so no database change); another photo or PDF of it updates the visit it was filed with and becomes its newer copy, the old one archived beside it (`AttachmentActions.keepAsNewerCopy`), as a bill does. The form says so before saving ("Factura e deja în dosar", with "Vezi factura"), and offers "E altă factură" when only the day, total and firm agree. A visit typed without an invoice is never taken for one already filed: there is no paper to compare. *Alternative:* match across the household by the firm and the number, which would pull a car's visit onto another car on a number alone.
- **Decided:** a visit filed again keeps what the form leaves empty (the number, the total, the kilometres, the firm, the rating), as a bill keeps what the new photo didn't read. Its lines are the form's. *Alternative:* the form's values only, which loses the kilometres when a second photo doesn't show them.
- **Decided:** visits filed before have no number; they are known by their day, total and firm, and there is no repair step. Reading the number again from each kept invoice at launch would be a guess on old photos, and a visit's day and total already tell two invoices apart in almost every case.
- **Decided:** the review doesn't file visits (an invoice for a section goes to the visit form, as before), so it has nothing to change here.
- **Decided:** "Am rezolvat" on a subscription's deadline with a photo of a bill files it as a bill, through the review's own steps (`CaptureFiler.logBill`, `finishBill`, the review's plan from `CaptureFiler.billPlan`): it fills what the subscription lacks (as a bill from the review, never rewriting what is there), is logged in Istoric once by `PaperIdentity`, its document is the bill's own entry, and its index is a reading. The sheet shows it first, in the review's words: "Am citit factura. O trec în istoricul „…”." or "Factura e deja în dosar" (with "E altă factură"), and the index's line. The deadline is paid as before, and a bill never makes a second reminder. A lower index asks before anything is written. This replaces "Only the review records one" in "A bill's meter index is a reading on its subscription": the index is still never recorded unseen. *Alternative:* keep "Am rezolvat" attaching only, which leaves the bill out of Istoric and its trend.
- **Decided:** a bill joined from the repair list (`SameBillTwiceStep`) leaves what filing it once leaves (`SameBillTwiceStep.Join`): one index, the first filed that counts in the subscription's series (a reading set aside after the person kept the old one doesn't), else the first filed, with every copy pointing to it; one *Factura de plătit*: a paid one (paid stays paid), else one another bill's papers use too, else the first made; the extra ones still running go, and a paid one stays as the record of a payment the person made. A bill an earlier version of the step joined with its extra index or reminder left is offered again, with its own wording ("Factura din … de la „…” are indexul sau termenul de plată de mai multe ori. Le las o singură dată?"). It still asks first, and runs in the repair list's one unit, as before. This replaces "A second reading or reminder an extra photo made stays as it is" in "Another photo of the same bill is the same bill". *Alternative:* keep the index of the copy that stays current (the newest), which would drop the one the person had answered for.
- **Why:** another photo of a visit's invoice made a second visit, a joined bill still showed two indexes or asked to be paid again, and a bill paid from "Am rezolvat" never reached Istoric.

### A card number printed next to other digits
- **Decided:** a card number printed next to other digits, with only a space or a dash between them ("12 4111 1111 1111 1111", a CNP then a card number, a card number then "27 lei"), is found inside the longer run by the "anywhere" rule (`PaymentCardNumbers.cardNumbers(in:)`): a part of the run that begins and ends where its groups do, tried from the left and the longest first at each place, taken when it starts like a card, has a card's length, is printed like one (one piece, groups of four with the last of 19 digits in three, American Express's 4-6-5 and Diners Club's 4-6-4) and passes the Luhn check. A whole run is taken as before (a misread check digit is forgiven when it is printed in fours); inside a longer run it isn't, since inside a long number printed in fours every part is grouped that way. Digits with nothing between them are one number, only ever taken whole, so a meter's or a contract's long code is never cut into a card number. The rule on a bank card (`strict`) already masked every run; its last four digits now come from the card number inside a longer run when there is one. Every user of the rule (the save, capture, search, the iPhone's search, a field's note, the repair list) gets it. *Alternatives:* every 14–19 digit window of a long run, separators or not, which would mask about one in ten long codes that happen to start like a card; only the leftmost or rightmost part, which misses a card between two other numbers.
- **Why:** a run of more than 19 digits was left whole, so a card number printed after a short code with one space between them was kept in full.

### Every save goes through the unit of work
- **Decided:** SwiftData's saving by itself (`autosaveEnabled`) is off for every context the app makes: the main context, which every screen and action uses, is turned off where the one container is made (`PersistenceController.makeContainer`), and a context of its own (a backup's writer, search, the coordinator's and Siri's readers, the store's copy that change times and undo read) is made only by `PersistenceController.context(in:)`. A change left on a context is written by the next unit of work, which keeps change times, masks card numbers and rolls back on failure. The architecture check rejects `ModelContext(`, `ModelContainer(` and `autosaveEnabled = true` anywhere else (`context-outside-persistence`). Tests' shared helpers make their contexts the same way. *Alternatives:* turn it off at each place a context is made, which the next one forgets (contexts SwiftData makes save by themselves unless told otherwise, not only the main one); keep it on and rely on every change being saved by an action anyway, which writes a change made outside an action without its change time or masking.
- **Why:** the unit of work does work before every save and takes everything back when one fails; a save SwiftData made by itself did neither.

### Card numbers are kept out where the unit of work saves
- **Decided:** a bank card's full number is masked in every record's words by one step the unit of work runs just before every save (`FreeText.mask`, beside the change times), the backup's own writer included. Words are what a person typed or a reader read: a section's name, notes, values and rows under Date importante, a deadline's title, notes, firm and reference, a visit's title, note and number, a line item's texts, a reading's name, source and notes, a firm's name, trades, phone, email, address and notes, a document's title, file name and text, and a category of one's own (its name, summary and the words in its definition). Each model lists which of its stored texts are words (`freeText`) and which never are (`machineText`: ids, keys, hashes, content types, pictures, the app's own rules), and `CardNumbersKeptOutTests` checks both lists against the schema, so a new property can't be left out. Only the last four digits stay ("•••• 1111"), by the same rule as before (`PaymentCardNumbers`). *Alternatives:* a call in each action (`AssetActions`, `HistoryActions`, `ProviderActions`, …), which every new action would have to remember; masking only on the way out (search, Spotlight, backups), which still keeps the number on the phone.
- **Decided:** the calls that masked at single doors (a section's fields in `AssetActions`, a document's text in `AttachmentActions` and capture's filing, a backup's values, rows and texts in `DossierImporter`) are removed: the save does the same for everything. Capture still masks its results before the review sees them (strict on a bank card), and search and Spotlight still mask what they show; those are about text on its way to a screen or out of the app, not about what is kept.
- **Decided:** a secret (a PIN field, a code row) is masked before it is sealed (`SecretFields.sealing`, `sealingFacts`): once sealed it is never opened to be rewritten, so that is the one place it can be.
- **Decided:** a field says under it, once the person leaves it, "Din numărul cardului păstrez doar ultimele patru cifre." when its text holds a card number (`DSTextField`, `DSSecretField`, through `DSValidation.keepingCardNumberOut`). A note, never a block; what was typed stays in the field as typed until saving. *Alternative:* mask the text in the field when the person leaves it, which changes what they see while they still edit; or a toast after saving, which comes after the fact.
- **Decided:** numbers an earlier version kept whole are offered in *Date de verificat*, one finding per record, never showing the number ("„Barca de pe lac” păstrează un număr de card întreg. Păstrez doar ultimele patru cifre?"; `CardNumbersStep`, at the end of the registry). It asks, as every repair that changes what the person sees. A record saved again for any other reason is masked by that save, asked or not. *Alternative:* mask them by themselves at launch, with no question.
- **Why:** the binder never holds a card number, from any path; one rule where everything is saved covers rows under Date importante, notes, titles and every action still to come, and a rule each action has to remember is one the next action forgets.

## 2026-11-09

### One merge rule for every record
- **Decided:** "newer" is the same for every kind of record: when it last changed, stored on the record (`updatedAt`). History entries, line items, readings and documents get one in database version 8, as sections, deadlines, firms and categories already had. A backup brought in with "Adaugă" takes the archive's copy of a record on both sides only when it changed later; on a tie the phone's copy stays. One rule in one place (`ArchiveMerge`, used by `DossierImporter` for every type, in both modes). Archives carry the new change times as optional fields. *Alternatives:* a fingerprint of the record's content, which tells two copies apart but not which is newer; the newest time of the record and its section, which makes every visit in a section look changed when only the section was.
- **Decided:** change times are kept by the unit of work as it saves, in one place (`ChangeTimes`): a record whose own values or links changed gets a new time, unless the step set one itself. Its own links are the records it points to and the lists it keeps itself (a firm's sections, a visit's firms), the side a backup carries; a list of the records that point at it isn't (a section doesn't change when it gets a visit). A document changes when one of its pages does. A step that leaves a record's time as it was, even by writing the same time again, is taken as giving none (only a frozen clock in a test can do that). Undo puts change times back, as before. *Alternatives:* every action setting the time itself, which is many paths and one forgotten makes a stale copy win; counting every link, which makes a section newer whenever it gets a visit, so a rename made on the other phone would be lost.
- **Decided:** records saved before version 8 get an unknown change time (`ChangeTime.unknown`, the oldest moment) through a lightweight migration, and so does a record from a backup that carries none. It is the same on every phone, so two copies nobody touched are never taken for a newer one, and any change made from now on is newer. *Alternatives:* the moment the phone updated, which differs per phone, so an untouched copy from a phone that updated later would overwrite a real change; the record's own date (a visit's day), which a visit dated ahead would make win over every later change.
- **Decided:** a merge never deletes. A record deleted on one phone comes back from a backup made before; doing better needs a record of deletions, which belongs to the sync design. *Alternative:* keep deletions now (a new kind of record and a format change) before sync is designed.
- **Why:** history entries, line items, readings and documents were added only when new, so a visit corrected on one phone never reached the other, while sections and deadlines followed their change times: two rules for one kind of question.

### What a newer version wrote, the rest
- **Decided:** a list stored as JSON keeps each entry this build can't read, raw, in its place, and writes it back unchanged (`KeptList`): a category's sections, deadlines and parts, and a section's rows under Date importante. Readable entries are edited as before; an unreadable one goes only when a readable one with the same key or id takes its place. *Alternative:* keep the whole list raw when one entry can't be read and show nothing of it, so a category of one's own couldn't be used or edited until the app is updated.
- **Decided:** a recurrence this build can't read (a type, a legal rule, a unit or an anchor it doesn't know) is kept as written (`RecurrenceRule.unreadable`) and behaves as one-off until the person picks a rule; a field keeps a type, role, default, derivation or condition it can't read, and reads as text without it. Kinds of history entries, readings and documents from a backup are kept as written. *Alternative:* the nearest rule this build knows (an unknown interval unit read as days), which reminds at the wrong time.
- **Decided:** the exits' rules reach what can't be read: a row or value with anything sealed in it, or a row marked secret, is left out of backups and never comes in; a card number in a row under Date importante, read or not, or in a field value this build can't read, is masked on the way in.
- **Why:** an unknown recurrence was read as one-off and backups wrote it that way, and one unreadable section or row made the whole list fall back to nothing, which the next save wrote back.

### Another photo of the same bill is the same bill
- **Decided:** a bill is known within its subscription (the provider and the contract, which `SubscriptionMatcher` already decided): by its invoice number when both bills have one (the same number, however it is written, is the same bill; another number is another bill, even on the same day for the same amount), else by its issue day and its total together; a bill with neither is never taken for another. One rule, `BillIdentity`, used by the filing plan (`CaptureFilingPlan.filedBill`), the review, the filer and the repair list. The same document filed again is still found by its own link, wherever it is now. *Alternative:* the provider and the number across the whole household, which would pull a bill from one home's subscription into another's on a number alone.
- **Decided:** the invoice number is kept on the history entry (`HistoryEntry.reference`, database version 7), not read back from its note. The note was text the app wrote for people ("Factura nr. …"); matching on it breaks the day the wording changes. Archives carry it as an optional field. *Alternative:* parse the note, with no database change.
- **Decided:** the new photo or PDF is the bill's newer copy: it is filed with the bill's entry, and the copy kept before is archived and points to it (`AttachmentActions.keepAsNewerCopy`, through the existing `supersede`), never deleted. Saving updates the entry with what was read now; what the new photo didn't read (its number, its total, its day) stays as it was. *Alternative:* a choice in the review between the new photo and the old one, which is one more question on every second photo.
- **Decided:** the review says so where the duplicate document notice was: "Factura e deja în dosar", where and from when, with "Vezi factura". Only when the match rests on the day and total (no number to compare) it offers "E altă factură", which files it as a new bill; a number or the same document is certain and offers no way out. *Alternative:* the way out every time, or never.
- **Decided:** a bill already logged whose reminder no longer runs (paid with "Gata", or deleted) never makes a second "Factura de plătit": the review says "Factura e deja în dosar, așa că nu îți amintesc încă o dată de ea." (`CaptureFilingPlan.Quiet.billFiledBefore`). This covers the same document filed again after paying too, which made a second reminder before. A reminder still running moves to a later corrected due day, as before.
- **Decided:** bills filed before version 7 get their number from the note the app wrote, by themselves, since the page says the same (`BillNumbersStep`); a bill logged more than once is joined only with the owner's OK (`SameBillTwiceStep`): the entry whose paper came last stays, the others' papers move to it (the newest current, the rest archived beside it) and the extra entries go. A second reading or reminder an extra photo made stays as it is. *Alternative:* join them silently, or leave them.
- **Why:** filing the PDF of a bill already photographed, or a second, sharper photo, doubled Istoric and the bill's trend, and after paying, its reminder. This answers the "Left" note in "A document filed again logs one entry".

### "Anulează" for the last steps
- **Decided:** deleting a firm from Agendă, deleting a category of one's own, removing the examples, a visit saved through capture and onboarding's own capture are undoable through the one receipt (`UnitOfWork.performUndoable`, `UndoReceipt`, `DSToast.undo` and `undoDeletion`). A deletion comes back with every link: a firm's sections, jobs, supplied parts and documents; a category's place on the binder; every example with what it held, and an example kept because it held something of the person's becomes an example again.
- **Decided:** deleting a firm, a category and the examples still asks first, as before ("Deleting a section or a document still asks first"); whether those questions can go now that the toast takes them back is the owner's call.
- **Decided:** a visit saved through capture or "Scriu eu" closes with "Vizita e în istoric." and "Anulează" on the shell's host, after its own summary with "Anulează vizita"; the invoice shared from another app waits hidden in De verificat until the toast is gone, by the same rule as a filing (`CaptureFlowResult.leaving`).
- **Decided:** onboarding shows its own toast, as a screen that stays: its full-screen cover hides the shell's host. Taken back, the photo step offers the photo again. *Alternative:* one toast host above every cover, in a window of its own like the lock's, which would serve any cover but is a second host to keep in step with the shell's. This replaces the "Kept" note on onboarding in "„Anulează” for every step, deletions included".
- **Kept:** deleting from Istoric is not undo.
- **Why:** these were the last steps that left the person with no way back but redoing it by hand.

### Questions are asked before anything is written
